The problem,\n27\tthe harness facts, and the recommended option are fixed; the design questions below are open.\n28\t\n29\t**Law.** `AGENTS.md`; `.claude/rules/names.md`, `typescript.md`, `architecture.md`, `patterns.md`,\n30\t`tests.md`, `workspace.md`, `portability.md`, `documentation.md`, `writing.md`, `quality.md`;\n31\t`.agents/orchestration.md`. It drives plan\n38\t  membership (`nameToHostArtifacts`, `src/core/compilers.ts:1512-1518`, ownership `presence`),\n39\t  staging (`stageHost`, `src/server/helpers.ts:1382-1535`, into `dist/host` with `manifest.json`),\n40\t  and the committed inventory (`stageInventory`, `src/server/helpers.ts:1562-1611`, into\n41\t  `host.json`). Both are\n78\tgit-ignored. The `.orkestrel/campaign/` folder holds a prior campaign's record; it is off-limits\n79\tand not part of this subject.\n80\t\n81\t## Design questions to rule\n82\t\n83\tRule on each, with the reasoning your lane's perspective supplies:\n84\t\n85\t1. **The membership split.** How the code expresses the distinction: instruction members are staged\n86\t   into `dist/host` and `host.json` (the `node_modules` fallback the pointer names) but leave the\n87\t   vendored-into-target plan; the tool surface stays staged and planned; pointer files are new\n88\t   content planned at the `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md` destinations. Name the constants, types, and\n89\t   helper changes exactly (naming law binds: UPPER_SNAKE_CASE `{QUALIFIER}_{NOUN}` constants,\n90\t   single-word entity members, named discriminants, boolean behavior switches, derive state).\n91\t2. **Pointer mechanism and content.** Where the pointer bytes live in scaffold's tree and how they\n92\t   stage (a new source directory with `source`-mapped destinations, a template, or computed\n93\t   content), and the exact pointer prose per `PROPOSAL.md:54-60`: plain prose naming\n94\t   `@orkestrel/scaffold` as authority, the sibling `../scaffold/` read when present, the\n95\t   `node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/` fallback otherwise, no `@path` import syntax.\n96\t   Writing law binds the prose.\n97\t3. **`.claude/settings.json`.** Stays vendored (the permissions floor is tool-read per repository)\n98\t   or leaves with the instruction set. `PROPOSAL.md:87-89` leaves this to design.\n99\t4. **Bridge files in targets.** `.codex/*`, `.cursor/*`, `.mcp.json` leave the vendored set per\n100\t   `PROPOSAL.md:81-89`, yet `PROPOSAL.md:61-64` says the bridges shrink to pointer paths. Rule\n101\t   whether a target keeps any bridge beyond the pointer pair, and what a Codex or Cursor session on\n102\t   a bare target reads.\n103\t5. **The catalog agent.** `.claude/agents/orkestrel.md` is written into targets by the catalog verb\n104\t   and polices the package table. Rule where it lands once `.claude/agents` leaves the vendored\n105\t   set, or whether it leaves too.\n106\t6. **Policy-test re-scope.** The vendored `tests/policy.test.ts` must pass in a target whose\n107\t   instruction trees are absent while still binding in scaffold's own tree. Rule the mechanism —\n108\t   pass-on-absence at the assertions named in the evidence, a scaffold-only registration, or\n109\t   another seam — without weakening what it proves where the trees exist.\n110\t7. **The orphan sweep.** Each target needs a one-time deletion of the superseded copies\n111\t   (`PROPOSAL.md:96-100`). Rule whether scaffold ships a mechanism (a verb, a repair extension) or\n112\t   the visit documents a manual step; mechanism-not-product-policy binds.\n113\t8. **Membership edges.** `scripts/*.sh` (bench hooks), `.agents/templates`, `.agents/transports`,\n114\t   `LICENSE`, `guides/guide.md`, `guides/scaffold.md`: instruction surface or tool surface, per\n115\t   `PROPOSAL.md:80-89` and what each governs.\n116\t9. **Documentation parity.** Which guide sections, README passages, and ROADMAP rows the change\n117\t   owns, and what `PROPOSAL.md` itself becomes at acceptance.\n118\t10. **Types-first order and units.** The bounded implementation units, their dependency order, one\n119\t    writer at a time, each with independently checkable acceptance criteria.\n120\t\n121\t## Unknowns\n122\t\n123\t- Whether the cloud harness honors `claudeMdExcludes` and per-root rule loading\n124\t  (`PROPOSAL.md:125-129`): unmeasurable from this session; treat as out of the code change's scope\n125\t  and report it as a standing unknown if your design depends on it.\n126\t- Whether any target consumes `dist/host` paths beyond `audit`/`repair`/`catalog`: report as\n127\t  unknown if load-bearing for your design.\n128\t\n129\t## Scope\n130\t\n131\t**Owned.** None — this is a read-only design lane. Return prose only.\n132\t\n133\t**Shared (report-only).** Every repository file.\n134\t\n135\t**Off-limits.** Editing anything; `.orkestrel/campaign/`; secrets (`.env*`, auth files, tokens).\n136\t\n137\t**Tools and limits.** Read, Grep, Glob only. No file writes, no shell.\n138\t\n139\t## Execution\n140\t\n141\tA native subagent, or a bench engine reading this brief inside its own CLI: perform the assignment\n142\tdirectly and spawn nothing. State which lane you held.\n143\t\n144\t## Output\n145\t\n146\tReturn exactly the `planner` return shape:\n147\t\n148\t- `Design`: the coherent contract — constants, types, helpers, staging, plan, pointer content,\n149\t  test re-scope, documentation — answering every numbered question.\n150\t- `Alternatives`: at most two real alternatives and why the design wins.\n151\t- `Units`: bounded work, each naming role AND engine, ownership, dependencies, and acceptance\n152\t  criteria.\n153\t- `Tensions`: judgment calls named for the other lane to challenge.\n154\t- `Risks`: what could invalidate the design and the evidence that settles each.\n155\t\n156\tDeliver it as your final message. Write no files.\n157\t\n158\t## Deviation contract\n159\t\n160\tA conflict between `PROPOSAL.md` and the law, or evidence that a numbered question rests on a false\n161\tpremise, stops the lane: report expected, found, exact evidence, and one short hypothesis. Where a\n162\tquestion is ancillary — a section's placement, a name between two lawful forms — decide, record,\n163\tand carry on.\n164\t\n165\t## Acceptance criteria\n166\t\n167\t1. Every numbered design question receives a ruling with its reasoning.\n168\t2. Every unit names role, engine, owned files, dependencies, and checkable acceptance criteria.\n169\t3. The design violates no non-negotiable in `AGENTS.md` and no named rule file.\n170\t4. The exit criterion for the campaign is stated: the enumerated capabilities whose closure ends\n171\t   it.\n172\t\n173\t## Review evidence\n174\t\n175\tThe subject is a design proposal: the proposal is `PROPOSAL.md`, the canon it must satisfy is\n176\t`AGENTS.md` plus the rule files named under Law, and the record of what motivated it is\n177\t`PROPOSAL.md:8-21` with the absorption distillate.\n178\t"}]},"uuid":"8ba0db5c-ad73-48b2-b1d1-442e54dd9705","timestamp":"2026-08-27T01:38:25.380Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"afff343c-f910-49a9-8bd5-f5edbbbd222b","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"f26af0ec-52c0-4923-8b7f-af34488a085d","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"1243f16d-def8-4fe9-8f90-73e8b336c933","agentId":"a85cb90a15fbe52ec","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01UQdR9XJnyLYgE7dUgrDi1u","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# PROPOSAL: scaffold as the single host for the instruction set\n2\t\n3\tStop propagating the instruction files into targets. Scaffold keeps the one canonical\n4\tcopy, every target carries a small pointer, and a session attaches scaffold beside the\n5\trepository it works. Researched and drafted on 2026-08-26; the owner rules on it in a\n6\tscaffold session.\n7\t\n8\t## The problem, measured\n9\t\n10\t- A Claude Code cloud session that attaches the whole fleet injects every checkout's\n11\t  memory set into the model context. The session panel on 2026-08-26 read 335.2k tokens\n12\t  of memory files — 33.5% of a 1M window — across 44 files: eleven repositories times\n13\t  the four files each root auto-loads.\n14\t- The per-repository bytes behind that reading, measured in this checkout: `CLAUDE.md`\n15\t  2,844, `AGENTS.md` 17,950, `.agents/orchestration.md` 63,388, and\n16\t  `.claude/rules/writing.md` 8,220 — roughly 92 KiB of always-loaded prose per\n17\t  repository, byte-identical across all of them.\n18\t- The damage exceeds the visible percentage. A non-fork subagent inherits the full\n19\t  memory hierarchy, so in that session an agent pinned to a smaller-window model failed\n20\t  to boot at all — every launch died on \"Prompt is too long\" before doing any work.\n21\t\n22\t## The harness facts, grounded\n23\t\n24\tEach fact carries its source; \"undocumented\" marks where the documentation is silent and\n25\tthe session must measure.\n26\t\n27\t- Claude Code loads `CLAUDE.md` from the working directory and every ancestor at launch,\n28\t  after managed policy and `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`; subtree copies load on reads into\n29\t  those subtrees (see the memory page, code.claude.com/docs/en/memory).\n30\t- Locally, `--add-dir` directories do **not** load memory by default; the\n31\t  `CLAUDE_CODE_ADDITIONAL_DIRECTORIES_CLAUDE_MD=1` variable opts in. Cloud multi-repo\n32\t  loading is undocumented, and the observed cloud behavior loads every attached root\n33\t  with no documented off switch.\n34\t- The `claudeMdExcludes` settings key skips memory files by path or glob at any settings\n35\t  layer (memory page). Whether the cloud harness honors it is undocumented.\n36\t- An `@path` import inlines content per importing file — deduplication across importers\n37\t  is undocumented, so assume none — and an import resolving outside the working\n38\t  directory triggers an approval dialog. A pointer written as an import would therefore\n39\t  recreate the duplication or stall on approval; a pointer must be plain prose the agent\n40\t  follows with a Read.\n41\t- Rules without `paths` frontmatter load at launch like `.claude/CLAUDE.md`; rules with\n42\t  `paths` frontmatter load when matching files are read (memory page).\n43\t- `node_modules` is never auto-loaded as memory. A copy there is present on disk, read\n44\t  on demand, and injected never — the right property for a fallback.\n45\t- A non-fork subagent receives every level of the memory hierarchy the main session\n46\t  loads (see the subagents page, code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents).\n47\t\n48\t## The design\n49\t\n50\t- Scaffold's own working tree stays exactly what it is: the canonical `AGENTS.md`,\n51\t  `CLAUDE.md`, `.agents/`, `.claude/`, `.codex/`, `.cursor/`, and `.mcp.json`. Editing\n52\t  them here is the whole change process — no propagation, no fleet churn, no drift, and\n53\t  `scaffold audit` stops policing copies that no longer exist.\n54\t- Every target carries small pointer files in their place. The pointer says, as prose:\n55\t  the coding and orchestration authority is `@orkestrel/scaffold`; when a scaffold\n56\t  checkout sits beside this repository, read `../scaffold/AGENTS.md` and\n57\t  `../scaffold/.agents/orchestration.md` before working; otherwise read the same files\n58\t  from `node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/`. Write the pointer pair as two\n59\t  small files (`CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md`) rather than a symlink — the documented\n60\t  `ln -s` trick breaks on Windows checkouts.\n61\t- The Codex and Cursor bridges already instruct by reference — `developer_instructions`\n62\t  in `.codex/config.toml` and the `alwaysApply` rule in `.cursor/rules/` both say \"read\n63\t  `AGENTS.md`, `.agents/orchestration.md`, …\" — so they shrink to the same pointer\n64\t  paths rather than changing shape.\n65\t- A session then attaches scaffold beside the repositories it works. Scaffold's memory\n66\t  loads once; each target contributes a pointer of a few hundred bytes. A whole-fleet\n67\t  session carries one canonical copy instead of eleven.\n68\t- Dispatch briefs name `/home/user/scaffold/AGENTS.md` and its siblings as the files an\n69\t  executor reads before working, which the brief template already parameterizes.\n70\t\n71\t## The migration, mechanically\n72\t\n73\tThe propagation surface is mapped; every pointer below was verified against this\n74\tcheckout on 2026-08-26.\n75\t\n76\t1. Split membership. `HOST_PATHS` (`src/core/constants.ts:124-159`) is one flat list\n77\t   that drives both staging into `dist/host` (`stageHost`,\n78\t   `src/server/helpers.ts:1382-1534`) and restoration into targets (`Materializer`,\n79\t   `src/bin/CLI.ts` `audit`/`repair`). Introduce the distinction the `inferGroup`\n80\t   labels already sketch (`src/core/helpers.ts:216-227`): the instruction members —\n81\t   `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.agents/*`, `.claude/agents`, `.claude/rules`,\n82\t   `.claude/skills`, `.codex/*`, `.cursor/*`, `.mcp.json` — leave the\n83\t   vendored-into-target set; the tool surface — editor and git dotfiles, oxlint,\n84\t   oxfmt, prettier ignore, `configs/`, `scripts/`, the policy tests, `LICENSE`,\n85\t   `guides/guide.md`, `guides/scaffold.md` — stays vendored. Rule during design\n86\t   whether `.claude/settings.json` stays vendored (the permissions floor is tool-read\n87\t   per repository) and whether the instruction files keep shipping in `dist/host` as\n88\t   the `node_modules` fallback while no longer being written into targets — the\n89\t   fallback in the pointer requires that they do.\n90\t2. Add the pointer files to the vendored set, so `repair` installs and polices them:\n91\t   the same destinations (`CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`), new small content, new digests —\n92\t   `repair` rewrites them in every target as stale entries.\n93\t3. Regenerate. `stageInventory` rewrites `host.json`, and\n94\t   `tests/config.test.ts:594-686` holds committed `host.json` equal to a fresh\n95\t   generation, so the membership change closes there.\n96\t4. Sweep the orphans. `repair` writes only missing and stale entries\n97\t   (`src/server/Materializer.ts:291-314`); a file removed from the inventory is\n98\t   unmanaged, not deleted. Each target needs a one-time deletion of the superseded\n99\t   full copies (`.agents/`, `.claude/agents`, `.claude/rules`, `.claude/skills`,\n100\t   `.codex/`, `.cursor/`, `.mcp.json`) in the same visit that re-pins and repairs.\n101\t5. (publishes ? `The ${name} package.` : `The ${name} application.`)\n1443\t\treturn [\n1444\t\t\t{\n1445\t\t\t\tpath: 'README.md',\n1446\t\t\t\tgroup: 'docs',\n1447\t\t\t\townership: 'birth',\n1448\t\t\t\torigin: 'template',\n1449\t\t\t\tcontent: fillTemplate(ARTIFACT_TEMPLATES.docs.readme, {\n1450\t\t\t\t\tpackage: name,\n1451\t\t\t\t\tdescription,\n1452\t\t\t\t}),\n1453\t\t\t},\n1454\t\t]\n1455\t}\n1456\t\n1457\t/**\n1458\t * Compile the blueprint-dependent orchestration artifacts.\n1459\t *\n1460\t * @param blueprint - The workspace specification.\n1461\t * @returns A vendor inventory script when vendors are declared, otherwise none.\n1462\t *\n1463\t * @remarks\n1464\t * A vendor name does not describe startup, readiness, or cleanup. The script\n1465\t * therefore records only the declared inventory and does not invent a service\n1466\t * runner or test project.\n1467\t */\n1468\texport function blueprintToOrchestrationArtifacts(\n1469\t\tblueprint: Blueprint,\n1470\t): readonly ContentArtifact[] {\n1471\t\tif (blueprint.vendors.length === 0) return []\n1472\t\tconst vendors = blueprint.vendors\n1473\t\t\t.map((vendor, index) => `\\t'${vendor}'${index === blueprint.vendors.length - 1 ? '' : ' \\\\'}`)\n1474\t\t\t.join('\\n')\n1475\t\treturn [\n1476\t\t\t{\n1477\t\t\t\tpath: SERVICE_SCRIPT_PATH,\n1478\t\t\t\tgroup: 'orchestration',\n1479\t\t\t\townership: 'birth',\n1480\t\t\t\torigin: 'template',\n1481\t\t\t\tcontent: fillTemplate(ARTIFACT_TEMPLATES.orchestration.service, { vendors }),\n1482\t\t\t},\n1483\t\t]\n1484\t}\n1485\t\n1486\t/**\n1487\t * Compile the vendored host artifacts a named workspace plans.\n1488\t *\n1489\t * @param name - The target workspace's own bare package name.\n1490\t * @returns One artifact per vendored path, in `HOST_PATHS` order.\n1491\t *\n1492\t * @remarks\n1493\t * Every artifact is claimed by presence, which is the strongest claim a pure\n1494\t * compile can make: core cannot read the vendored data root, so it cannot carry\n1495\t * the bytes a content claim would have to be checked against. Reading that root\n1496\t * is what promotes the ones scaffold owns the bytes of.\n1497\t *\n1498\t * `source` is left absent because it falls back to `path`, and every vendored\n1499\t * path is stored under the name it is written to. The group comes from\n1500\t * {@link inferGroup}, so a vendored path and a foreign path found in a target\n1501\t * are classified by one rule and a plan never disagrees with the audit beside\n1502\t * it.\n1503\t *\n1504\t * @example\n1505\t * ```ts\n1506\t * import { nameToHostArtifacts } from '@orkestrel/scaffold'\n1507\t *\n1508\t * nameToHostArtifacts('router').some((artifact) => artifact.path === 'AGENTS.md') // true\n1509\t * nameToHostArtifacts('router').some((artifact) => artifact.path === 'guides/router.md') // false\n1510\t * ```\n1511\t */\n1512\texport function nameToHostArtifacts(name: string): readonly Artifact[] {\n1513\t\treturn selectHostPaths(HOST_PATHS, name).map((path): Artifact => ({\n1514\t\t\tpath,\n1515\t\t\tgroup: inferGroup(path),\n1516\t\t\townership: 'presence',\n1517\t\t\torigin: 'host',\n1518\t\t}))\n1519\t}\n1520\t\n1521\t/**\n1522\t * Replace the content of every drafted artifact an override names.\n1523\t *\n1524\t * @param artifacts - The drafted artifacts.\n1525\t * @param overrides - The blueprint's overrides.\n1526\t * @returns The artifacts with each matching override applied, in input order.\n1527\t *\n1528\t * @remarks\n1529\t * An override replaces an artifact's whole content and never merges into it.\n1530\t * A host-origin artifact carries no content to replace, so it passes through\n1531\t * untouched; which overrides are legal at all is\n1532\t * {@link overridesToQuestions}'s answer, so an illegal one is reported rather\n1533\t * than silently dropped here.\n1534\t *\n1535\t * @example\n1536\t * ```ts\n1537\t * import { applyOverrides, blueprintToConfigArtifacts, createBlueprint } from '@orkestrel/scaffold'\n1538\t *\n1539\t * const artifacts = blueprintToConfigArtifacts(createBlueprint('router', { src: ['core'] }))\n1540\t *\n1541\t * applyOverrides(artifacts, [{ path: 'README.md', content: '# Title\\n' }])\n1542\t * ```\n1543\t */\n1544\texport function applyOverrides(\n1545\t\tartifacts: readonly Artifact[],\n1546\t\toverrides: readonly Override[],\n1547\t): readonly Artifact[] {\n1548\t\tif (overrides.length === 0) return artifacts\n1549\t\tconst replacements = new Map(overrides.map((override) => [override.path, override.content]))\n1550\t\treturn artifacts.map((artifact) => {\n1551\t\t\tif (artifact.origin === 'host') return artifact\n1552\t\t\tconst content = replacements.get(artifact.path)\n1553\t\t\treturn content === undefined ? artifact : { ...artifact, content }\n1554\t\t})\n1555\t}\n1556\t\n1557\t/**\n1558\t * Replace declared dependency ranges in package manifest text.\n1559\t *\n1560\t * @param manifest - The manifest text to compile.\n1561\t * @param pins - The runtime and development names and replacement ranges.\n1562\t * @returns The manifest with every matching writable value replaced, or\n1563\t * `undefined` when a name has no quoted declaration in its named section.\n1564\t *\n1565\t * @remarks\n1566\t * The compiler replaces values in place instead of serializing the manifest,\n1567\t * so description, keywords, scripts, key order, indentation, and every byte\n1568\t * outside the named ranges survive. Runtime pins apply only to `dependencies`,\n1569\t * and development pins apply only to `devDependencies`. The compiler never\n1570\t * reads or writes `peerDependencies` or `peerDependenciesMeta`. An override or\n1571\t * resolution with the same name stays untouched.\n1572\t *\n1573\t * @example\n1574\t * ```ts\n1575\t * import { replaceManifestRanges } from '@orkestrel/scaffold'\n1576\t *\n1577\t * const manifest = '{\"devDependencies\":{\"typescript\":\"^6\"}}\\n'\n1578\t * replaceManifestRanges(manifest, {\n1579\t *   runtime: [],"}]},"uuid":"9b75effb-8b31-428c-b60c-b0310286c7d8","timestamp":"2026-08-27T01:38:32.651Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"8c5c328c-c344-4cce-8949-1a81dab178eb","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"c897f3ef-07e8-4006-9f40-678baa244a37","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"1243f16d-def8-4fe9-8f90-73e8b336c933","agentId":"a85cb90a15fbe52ec","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012vw4esV7tFnMpEpxs2tAiZ","type":"tool_result","content":"330\t * guide mirror is presence-owned: repair restores one that is absent and never\n331\t * replaces one that is present.\n332\t */\n333\texport type Mirror =\n334\t\t| {\n335\t\t\t\treadonly name: string\n336\t\t\t\treadonly path: string\n337\t\t\t\treadonly lookup: 'found'\n338\t\t\t\treadonly content: string\n339\t\t\t\treadonly observed?: string\n340\t\t\t\treadonly note?: never\n341\t\t  }\n342\t\t| {\n343\t\t\t\treadonly name: string\n344\t\t\t\treadonly path: string\n345\t\t\t\treadonly lookup: 'missing' | 'unmatched' | 'failed'\n346\t\t\t\treadonly note: string\n347\t\t\t\treadonly observed?: string\n348\t\t\t\treadonly content?: never\n349\t\t  }\n350\t\n351\t/** The fields every planned file carries. */\n352\texport interface ArtifactBase {\n353\t\treadonly path: string\n354\t\treadonly group: Group\n355\t\treadonly ownership: Ownership\n356\t\treadonly environment?: Environment\n357\t}\n358\t\n359\t/**\n360\t * A file byte-copied from the vendored data root, planned before its bytes are read.\n361\t *\n362\t * @remarks\n363\t * `source` falls back to `path` when absent. The pure core face cannot read the\n364\t * vendored root, so a plan it compiles alone claims only that these files\n365\t * exist. That is why the ownership here is narrowed away from `content`: a\n366\t * claim over bytes nobody has read is a claim that cannot be checked. Reading\n367\t * the vendored root turns the ones scaffold owns the bytes of into\n368\t * {@link HydratedArtifact}. Workspace-owned paths and paths whose bytes belong\n369\t * to another verb stay plain host artifacts, because this writer claims only\n370\t * their presence.\n371\t */\n372\texport interface HostArtifact extends ArtifactBase {\n373\t\treadonly origin: 'host'\n374\t\treadonly ownership: 'presence' | 'birth'\n375\t\treadonly source?: string\n376\t\treadonly hex?: never\n377\t\treadonly content?: never\n378\t}\n379\t\n380\t/**\n381\t * A vendored file whose exact bytes have been read, so its content can be compared.\n382\t *\n383\t * @remarks\n384\t * `hex` is the canonical lowercase byte pairs of the vendored source. It is\n385\t * required, which is what makes content ownership honest: every artifact\n386\t * claiming a byte comparison carries the bytes that comparison needs. Hydration\n387\t * leaves a guide-mirror pointer and the catalog agent as plain host artifacts,\n388\t * because another verb owns those bytes.\n389\t */\n390\texport interface HydratedArtifact extends ArtifactBase {\n391\t\treadonly origin: 'host'\n392\t\treadonly ownership: 'content'\n393\t\treadonly source?: string\n394\t\treadonly hex: string\n395\t\treadonly content?: never\n396\t}\n397\t\n398\t/** A text file produced by the template or computed compilation path. */\n399\texport interface ContentArtifact extends ArtifactBase {\n400\t\treadonly origin: 'template' | 'computed'\n401\t\treadonly content: string\n402\t\treadonly hex?: never\n403\t\treadonly source?: never\n404\t}\n405\t\n406\t/**\n407\t * One file in a plan, discriminated by how its content is produced and what scaffold claims of it.\n408\t *\n409\t * @remarks\n410\t * Every branch that claims `content` ownership carries the bytes to back it:\n411\t * a hydrated artifact through `hex`, a template or computed artifact through\n412\t * `content`. No branch can claim a byte comparison it cannot perform.\n413\t */\n414\texport type Artifact = HostArtifact | HydratedArtifact | ContentArtifact\n415\t\n416\t/** Exact lowercase hexadecimal target bytes keyed by artifact-relative path. */\n417\texport type Snapshot = Readonly<Record<string, string>>\n418\t\n419\t/**\n420\t * The compiled, ordered artifact list and the selection it covers.\n421\t *\n422\t * @remarks\n423\t * `hash` is the plan's content identity and is absent until the pin stage\n424\t * fills it. An artifact at `package.json` carries `birth` ownership because the\n425\t * compiler emits the manifest that way. A plan claiming another ownership at\n426\t * that path contradicts the compiler and is outside this contract.\n427\t */\n428\texport interface Plan {\n429\t\treadonly blueprint: Blueprint\n430\t\treadonly groups: readonly Group[]\n431\t\treadonly artifacts: readonly Artifact[]\n432\t\treadonly hash?: string\n433\t}\n434\t\n435\t/**\n436\t * One drift verdict against a target path.\n437\t *\n438\t * @remarks\n439\t * `observed` carries the destination's exact bytes and is the precondition the\n440\t * mutation is held to: a write that replaces stale bytes and a deletion that\n441\t * removes a foreign file each fail when the destination no longer matches what\n442\t * the finding recorded. Both therefore require it, and the requirement is in\n443\t * the type rather than in prose, because a deletion that cannot bind to what\n444\t * the audit showed is the one thing the destructive verb must never do. A\n445\t * missing destination has no bytes to record. An aligned one may have gone\n446\t * uncompared, which is what a birth-owned path always does, so it records\n447\t * bytes only where they were actually read. Every planned finding carries its\n448\t * artifact's {@link Ownership}, so a consumer reads what scaffold claims at the\n449\t * path from the finding itself. A foreign finding has no planned artifact and\n450\t * therefore no ownership.\n451\t *\n452\t * Which combinations of `ownership`, `drift`, and `observed` a real audit\n453\t * produces is {@link inferDrift}'s law, not this type's. A birth-owned path is\n454\t * never compared and is always aligned, a presence-owned path compares existence\n455\t * only, and bytes are recorded only where they were read. This shape therefore\n456\t * admits a combination that law never produces — a birth-owned path reported\n457\t * stale is the plainest one — and it admits it deliberately: restating the\n458\t * comparison's case analysis here would be a second copy of it, able to disagree\n459\t * with the one that decides. What closes the gap is the writer: `repair` and\n460\t * `remove` re-derive every verdict themselves and refuse a caller's audit that\n461\t * does not match, so a verdict the comparison could not have reached is refused\n462\t * rather than acted on.\n463\t */\n464\texport type Finding =\n465\t\t| {\n466\t\t\t\treadonly path: string\n467\t\t\t\treadonly group: Group\n468\t\t\t\treadonly ownership: Ownership\n469\t\t\t\treadonly drift: 'stale'\n470\t\t\t\treadonly observed: string\n471\t\t  }\n472\t\t| {\n473\t\t\t\treadonly path: string\n474\t\t\t\treadonly group: Group\n475\t\t\t\treadonly ownership?: never\n476\t\t\t\treadonly drift: 'foreign'\n477\t\t\t\treadonly observed: string\n478\t\t  }\n479\t\t| {\n480\t\t\t\treadonly path: string\n481\t\t\t\treadonly group: Group\n482\t\t\t\treadonly ownership: Ownership\n483\t\t\t\treadonly drift: 'missing'\n484\t\t\t\treadonly observed?: never\n485\t\t  }\n486\t\t| {\n487\t\t\t\treadonly path: string\n488\t\t\t\treadonly group: Group\n489\t\t\t\treadonly ownership: Ownership\n490\t\t\t\treadonly drift: 'aligned'\n491\t\t\t\treadonly observed?: string\n492\t\t  }\n493\t\n494\t/**\n495\t * The whole comparison of a plan against a target's current content.\n496\t *\n497\t * @remarks\n498\t * A blocking question means the gate refused the blueprint, so `findings` is\n499\t * empty and says nothing about the target. Tallies are not stored: count\n500\t * `findings` by `drift` or `ownership`. The finding bound is the sum of the\n501\t * separately bounded plan artifacts and snapshot paths.\n502\t */\n503\texport interface Audit {\n504\t\treadonly findings: readonly Finding[]\n505\t\treadonly questions: readonly Question[]\n506\t}\n507\t\n508\t/**\n509\t * One validation issue raised against a blueprint or a plan."}]},"uuid":"1eaf0e86-a97b-4443-91b1-fcb5cc8dd6ce","timestamp":"2026-08-27T01:38:32.820Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"c897f3ef-07e8-4006-9f40-678baa244a37","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

That contract owns **target**\n1348\t * writes: it materializes a compiled plan into a consumer's workspace, binds\n1349\t * every destination to what the caller observed, and rolls a failed commit back.\n1350\t * This reads this package's own checkout at build time and fills its own build\n1351\t * output. Different direction, different lifetime, no consumer target involved,\n1352\t * so they do not overlap and neither one belongs inside the other.\n1353\t *\n1354\t * Staging is plain rather than transactional for the same reason. A\n1355\t * `WriteTransaction` exists to hold a directory that already holds work\n1356\t * still; a build output holds nothing, is deleted whole before every build, and\n1357\t * has no concurrent reader. A partial root\n1364\t * is not detectably partial: it fails later, in a consumer's terminal, on\n1365\t * whichever path the plan reached first. Refusing here fails the build that\n1366\t * produced it, where the maintainer can act, and it names every missing path at\n1367\t * once. entries\n1535\t}\n1536\t\n1537\t/**\n1538\t * Stages the committed inventory of the files a vendored host carries.\n1539\t *\n1540\t * @param checkout - The checkout whose vendored paths are (path.startsWith('src/') || path.startsWith('app/')) return 'source'\n220\t\tif (path.startsWith('tests/')) return 'tests'\n221\t\tif (path.startsWith('guides/')) return 'guides'\n222\t\tif (path.startsWith('docs/')) return 'docs'\n223\t\t// The licence and the root instruction documents are the workspace's own prose.\n224\t\tif (path === 'LICENSE') return 'docs'\n225\t\tif (!path.includes('/') && path.endsWith('.md')) return 'docs'\n226\t\treturn 'configs'\n227\t}\n228\t\n229\t/**\n230\t * Serialize one string as a single-quoted TypeScript literal.\n231\t *\n232\t * serializeTypeScriptString(\"it's\") // `'it\\\\'s'`\n246\t * ```\n247\t */\n248\texport function serializeTypeScriptString(value: string): string {\n249\t\tlet serialized = \"'\"\n250\t\tfor (let index = 0; index < value.length; index += 1) {\n251\t\t\tconst code = value.charCodeAt(index)\n252\t\t\tconst character = value.charAt(index)\n253\t\t\tif (character === \"'\") serialized += \"\\\\'\"\n254\t\t\telse if (character === '\\\\') serialized += '\\\\\\\\'\n255\t\t\telse if (character === '\\b') serialized += '\\\\b'\n256\t\t\telse if (character === '\\f') serialized += '\\\\f'\n257\t\t\telse if (character === '\\n') serialized += '\\\\n'\n258\t\t\telse if (character === '\\r') serialized += '\\\\r'\n259\t\t\telse if (character === '\\t') serialized += '\\\\t'\n260\t\t\telse if (code === 0x2028) serialized += '\\\\u2028'\n261\t\t\telse if (code === 0x2029) serialized += '\\\\u2029'\n262\t\t\telse if (code < 0x20 || (code >= 0xd800 && code <= 0xdfff)) {\n263\t\t\t\tconst next = value.charCodeAt(index + 1)\n264\t\t\t\tif (code >= 0xd800 && code <= 0xdbff && next >= 0xdc00 && next <= 0xdfff) {\n265\t\t\t\t\tserialized += content.replaceAll(${pattern}, ${specifier})`\n356\t\tif (matchesPrintWidth(joined)) return joined\n357\t\treturn [\n358\t\t\t'\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t? content.replaceAll(',\n359\t\t\t`\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t${pattern},`,\n360\t\t\t`\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t${specifier},`,\n361\t\t\t'\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t)',\n362\t\t].join('\\n')\n363\t}\n364\t\n365\t/**\n366\t * Select the host paths a named workspace vendors.\n367\t *\n368\t * @param paths - The candidate host paths, in their declared order.\n369\t * @param name - The target workspace's own bare package name.\n370\t * @returns Every candidate except the workspace's own guide, in input order.\n371\t *\n372\t * @remarks\n373\t * `HOST_PATHS` is a candidate set rather than a plan, because a workspace never\n374\t * mirrors its own guide: that file is the workspace's own product, and vendoring\n375\t * it would have the target overwrite its guide with the copy it published.\n376\t *\n377\t * @example\n378\t * ```ts\n379\t * import { HOST_PATHS, selectHostPaths } from '@orkestrel/scaffold'\n380\t *\n381\t * selectHostPaths(HOST_PATHS, 'scaffold').includes('guides/scaffold.md') // false\n382\t * ```\n383\t */\n384\texport function selectHostPaths(paths: readonly string[], name: string): readonly string[] {\n385\t\tconst guide = nameToGuide(name)\n386\t\treturn paths.filter((path) => path !== guide)\n387\t}\n388\t\n389\t/**\n390\t * Select the groups a compile covers, in plan order.\n391\t *\n392\t * @param groups - The requested selection; every group when absent.\n393\t * @returns The requested groups in `GROUPS` order, without repeats.\n394\t *\n395\t * @remarks\n396\t * A caller's selection is data, so it arrives in whatever order and with\n397\t * whatever repeats the caller wrote. {"parentUuid":"0c587408-9160-437d-9a40-70de97b8957b","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"1243f16d-def8-4fe9-8f90-73e8b336c933","agentId":"a85cb90a15fbe52ec","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018h1N84Qe2djYtoeipwmU5V","type":"tool_result","content":"150\t * foreign path found in a target are both classified through here, so a new\n151\t * harness directory is admitted once in `ORCHESTRATION_PATH_PREFIXES` and every\n152\t * caller follows.\n153\t *\n154\t * @example\n155\t * ```ts\n156\t * import { matchesOrchestrationPath } from '@orkestrel/scaffold'\n157\t *\n158\t * matchesOrchestrationPath('.claude/rules/names.md') // true\n159\t * matchesOrchestrationPath('.mcp.json') // true\n160\t * matchesOrchestrationPath('.oxlintrc.json') // false\n161\t * ```\n162\t */\n163\texport function matchesOrchestrationPath(path: string): boolean {\n164\t\tif (ORCHESTRATION_PATH_NAMES.includes(path)) return true\n165\t\treturn ORCHESTRATION_PATH_PREFIXES.some((prefix) => path.startsWith(prefix))\n166\t}\n167\t\n168\t/**\n169\t * Checks whether another surface owns the vendored bytes at a path.\n170\t *\n171\t * @param path - The target-relative vendored path to test.\n172\t * @returns `true` for the catalog agent file and for a Markdown guide mirror;\n173\t * `false` otherwise.\n174\t *\n175\t * @remarks\n176\t * The materializer keeps these paths presence-owned because the catalog or\n177\t * mirror surface writes their bytes. A live host fill therefore keeps the\n178\t * installed floor's bytes for them and requests only the host-owned paths.\n179\t *\n180\t * @example\n181\t * ```ts\n182\t * import { isDeferredPath } from '@orkestrel/scaffold'\n183\t *\n184\t * isDeferredPath('guides/router.md') // true\n185\t * isDeferredPath('AGENTS.md') // false\n186\t * ```\n187\t */\n188\texport function isDeferredPath(path: string): boolean {\n189\t\treturn path === CATALOG_AGENT_PATH || (path.startsWith('guides/') && path.endsWith('.md'))\n190\t}\n191\t\n192\t/**\n193\t * Infer the {@link Group} a path belongs to.\n194\t *"}]},"uuid":"74766833-e5fa-42c2-8f66-14e76c79cf41","timestamp":"2026-08-27T01:38:55.455Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0c587408-9160-437d-9a40-70de97b8957b","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"d970ec21-758d-43b2-b4ec-dabd3d12539f","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"1243f16d-def8-4fe9-8f90-73e8b336c933","agentId":"a85cb90a15fbe52ec","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01BEGtiHxrkcvK6xbnNePAuQ","type":"tool_result","content":"100\t\n101\t/**\n102\t * Test whether a target-relative path is one no verb may delete.\n103\t *\n104\t * @param path - The target-relative path to classify.\n105\t * @returns `true` when the path must survive every verb this package runs.\n106\t *\n107\t * @remarks\n108\t * The deletion deny-list, stated as a rule over paths rather than as a list of\n109\t * directories. It is the inversion the contract asks for: the candidate set\n110\t * is re-derived from the plan and narrowed by what git tracks, and the audit\n111\t * must agree with that derivation rather than supply the set itself.\n112\t * Git metadata is protected because losing history is not a repair,\n113\t * and a target's own `src` and `app` trees are protected because a\n114\t * workspace's source is the one thing scaffold never plans and never owns. A\n115\t * plan the compiler emits never maps a protected root, so this guard exists\n116\t * for the caller-authored plan a consumer can still supply.\n117\t *\n118\t * @example\n119\t * ```ts\n120\t * import { matchesProtectedPath } from '@orkestrel/scaffold/server'\n121\t *\n122\t * matchesProtectedPath('src/core/index.ts') // true\n123\t * matchesProtectedPath('.git/config') // true\n124\t * matchesProtectedPath('.claude/agents/rogue.md') // false\n125\t * ```\n126\t */\n127\texport function matchesProtectedPath(path: string): boolean {\n128\t\tconst normalized = path.replaceAll('\\\\', '/')\n129\t\tif (matchesGitPath(normalized)) return true\n130\t\tif (normalized === 'src' || normalized === 'app') return true\n131\t\treturn normalized.startsWith('src/') || normalized.startsWith('app/')\n132\t}\n133\t\n134\t/**\n135\t * Test whether a path names local configuration or a credential.\n136\t *\n137\t * @param path - The path to classify; either separator is read.\n138\t * @returns `true` when the path must never be copied into a vendored host.\n139\t *\n140\t * @remarks\n141\t * The vendoring deny-list. A host root is staged from a real checkout, so the\n142\t * refusal is stated over the path rather than over the file's content: a\n143\t * credential is recognizable by where it sits and what it is called long before\n144\t * anything reads it. Git metadata is included through\n145\t * {@link matchesGitPath}, so one call answers the whole question and no caller\n146\t * has to remember to ask twice.\n147\t *\n148\t * @example\n149\t * ```ts\n150\t * import { matchesSensitivePath } from '@orkestrel/scaffold/server'\n151\t *\n152\t * matchesSensitivePath('.npmrc') // true\n153\t * matchesSensitivePath('.claude/settings.local.json') // true\n154\t * matchesSensitivePath('.claude/settings.json') // false\n155\t * ```\n156\t */\n157\texport function matchesSensitivePath(path: string): boolean {\n158\t\tconst normalized = path.replaceAll('\\\\', '/')\n159\t\tif (matchesGitPath(normalized)) return true\n160\t\treturn /(?:^|\\/)(?:(?:\\.ssh|\\.aws|\\.azure|\\.docker|\\.kube|\\.gnupg|\\.env(?:\\.[^/]*)?)(?:\\/|$)|(?:\\.npmrc|\\.pypirc|\\.netrc|\\.git-credentials|settings\\.local\\.json|auth\\.json|credentials(?:\\.json)?|application_default_credentials\\.json|id_rsa|id_ed25519|kubeconfig)$|\\.config\\/(?:gh|gcloud)(?:\\/|$)|\\.local\\/share\\/keyrings(?:\\/|$)|[^/]*service-account[^/]*\\.json$|[^/]*\\.(?:jks|key|p12|pem|pfx|pkcs12)$)/i.test(\n161\t\t\tnormalized,\n162\t\t)\n163\t}\n164\t\n165\t/**\n166\t * Test whether a vendored path is one a target receives executable.\n167\t *\n168\t * @param path - The target-relative path to classify; either separator is read.\n169\t * @returns `true` when the path is declared in {@link EXECUTABLE_PATHS}.\n170\t *\n171\t * @remarks\n172\t * The declaration is the whole answer, and deliberately so. Reading the staging\n173\t * host's mode instead makes the manifest depend on where the package was built:\n174\t * Windows carries no executable bit, so a host staged there declares every entry\n175\t * non-executable and every target it later fills receives hooks at `0644`. One\n176\t * checkout stages one manifest on every host because this predicate never\n177\t * consults the filesystem.\n178\t *\n179\t * @example\n180\t * ```ts\n181\t * import { matchesExecutablePath } from '@orkestrel/scaffold/server'\n182\t *\n183\t * matchesExecutablePath('scripts/codex.sh') // true\n184\t * matchesExecutablePath('scripts\\\\deps.sh') // true\n185\t * matchesExecutablePath('AGENTS.md') // false\n186\t * ```\n187\t */\n188\texport function matchesExecutablePath(path: string): boolean {\n189\t\treturn EXECUTABLE_PATHS.includes(path.replaceAll('\\\\', '/'))\n190\t}\n191\t\n192\t/**\n193\t * Project a target-relative path to the storage name a vendored host holds it under.\n194\t *\n195\t * @param path - The target-relative path the file is written to.\n196\t * @returns The storage name beneath the host root.\n197\t *\n198\t * @remarks\n199\t * A staged host is a plain directory that npm packs, and npm's own ignore rules\n200\t * would drop a leading-dot entry from the tarball. So every dot that opens a\n201\t * segment comes off, and a dotted file at the root moves under `dotfiles/` to\n202\t * keep it from colliding with an undotted sibling of the same name. The mapping\n203\t * is one direction only: a staged host's manifest records the destination each\n204\t * storage name answers for, so the reader never re-derives this.\n205\t *\n206\t * @example\n207\t * ```ts\n208\t * import { pathToStorage } from '@orkestrel/scaffold/server'\n209\t *\n210\t * pathToStorage('.gitignore') // 'dotfiles/gitignore'\n211\t * pathToStorage('.claude/rules/names.md') // 'claude/rules/names.md'\n212\t * pathToStorage('AGENTS.md') // 'AGENTS.md'\n213\t * ```\n214\t */\n215\texport function pathToStorage(path: string): string {\n216\t\tconst segments = path.split('/')\n217\t\tif (segments.length === 1) {\n218\t\t\treturn path.startsWith('.') ? `dotfiles/${path.slice(1)}` : path\n219\t\t}\n220\t\treturn segments.map((segment) => (segment.startsWith('.') ? segment.slice(1) : segment)).join('/')\n221\t}\n222\t\n223\t/**\n224\t * Compute the SHA-256 digest of text.\n225\t *\n226\t * @param content - The text to digest.\n227\t * @returns Sixty-four lowercase hexadecimal digits.\n228\t *\n229\t * @remarks"}]},"uuid":"e49daf3c-696b-4eb7-9ac8-1586d5bfeb13","timestamp":"2026-08-27T01:39:31.881Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d970ec21-758d-43b2-b4ec-dabd3d12539f","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

The raw root instruction text.\n1629\t * @returns Each backticked first cell beneath the rule map heading, in table order.\n1630\t */\n1631\texport function readPolicyRuleMap(content: string): readonly string[] {\n1632\t\tconst lines = content.replaceAll('\\r\\n', '\\n').split('\\n')\n1633\t\tconst heading = lines.indexOf(POLICY_RULE_MAP_HEADING)\n1634\t\tif (heading === -1) return []\n1635\t\tconst paths: string[] = []\n1636\t\tfor (let index = heading + 1; index < lines.length; index += 1) {\n1637\t\t\tconst line = lines[index]\n1638\t\t\tif (line === undefined || line.startsWith('## ')) break\n1639\t\t\tconst cell = line.match(/^\\|\\s*`([^`]+)`\\s*\\|/u)?.[1]\n1640\t\t\tif (cell !== undefined) paths.push(normalizePolicyPath(cell))\n1641\t\t}\n1642\t\treturn paths\n1643\t}\n1644\t\n1645\t/**\n1646\t * Inspect the discovered rule family against the root instruction file's rule map.\n1647\t *\n1648\t * A workspace with no rule file has no rule map to keep, so the population is empty there.\n1649\t *\n1650\t * @param root - The workspace root to inspect.\n1651\t * @returns Every unregistered rule file, then every row resolving to no file.\n1652\t */\n1653\texport function inspectPolicyRuleMap(root: string): readonly PolicyViolation[] {\n1654\t\tconst directory = resolvePolicyDirectory(root, POLICY_RULE_ROOT)\n1655\t\tif (directory === undefined) return []\n1656\t\tconst rules = readdirSync(directory, { withFileTypes: true })\n1657\t\t\t.filter((entry) => entry.isFile() && entry.name.endsWith('.md'))\n1658\t\t\t.map((entry) => `${POLICY_RULE_ROOT}/${entry.name}`)\n1659\t\t\t.sort()\n1660\t\tif (rules.length === 0) return []\n1661\t\tconst content = isPolicyFile(root, POLICY_RULE_MAP_FILE)\n1662\t\t\t? readFileSync(join(root, POLICY_RULE_MAP_FILE), 'utf8')\n1663\t\t\t: ''\n1664\t\tconst registered = new Set(readPolicyRuleMap(content))\n1665\t\tconst violations: PolicyViolation[] = []\n1666\t\tfor (const rule of rules) {\n1667\t\t\tif (!registered.has(rule)) {\n1668\t\t\t\tviolations.push(createPolicyViolation('rules', rule, 'the rule map names every rule file'))\n1669\t\t\t}\n1670\t\t}\n1671\t\tfor (const path of registered) {\n1672\t\t\tif (!isPolicyFile(root, path)) {\n1673\t\t\t\tviolations.push(\n1674\t\t\t\t\tcreatePolicyViolation('rules', path, 'every rule-map row resolves to a rule file'),\n1675\t\t\t\t)\n1676\t\t\t}\n1677\t\t}\n1678\t\treturn violations\n1679\t}\n1680\t\n1681\t/**\n1682\t * Inspect an explicit path population for a name a Windows checkout cannot hold.\n1683\t *\n1684\t * Each path is read through its own final segment, because the population lists every directory as\n1685\t * its own entry. A Windows host refuses the reserved characters and folds a case collision into one\n1686\t * file, so those two boundaries are proven from a path population rather than from written files.\n1687\t *\n1688\t * @param paths - The workspace-relative paths to inspect.\n1689\t * @returns Every unusable-name and case-collision violation in path order.\n1690\t */\n1691\texport function inspectPolicyFilenamePaths(paths: readonly string[]): readonly PolicyViolation[] {\n1692\t\tconst violations: PolicyViolation[] = []\n1693\t\tconst folded = new Map<string, string>()\n1694\t\tfor (const candidate of paths) {\n1695\t\t\tconst path = normalizePolicyPath(candidate)\n1696\t\t\tconst segment = basename(path)\n1697\t\t\tconst [stem = ''] = segment.split('.')\n1698\t\t\tif (POLICY_RESERVED_NAMES.includes(stem.toLowerCase())) {\n1699\t\t\t\tviolations.push(\n1700\t\t\t\t\tcreatePolicyViolation(\n1701\t\t\t\t\t\t'portability',\n1702\t\t\t\t\t\tpath,\n1703\t\t\t\t\t\t'path segments avoid the names Windows reserves',\n1704\t\t\t\t\t),\n1705\t\t\t\t)\n1706\t\t\t}\n1707\t\t\tif (POLICY_RESERVED_PATTERN.test(segment)) {\n1708\t\t\t\tviolations.push(\n1709\t\t\t\t\tcreatePolicyViolation(\n1710\t\t\t\t\t\t'portability',\n1711\t\t\t\t\t\tpath,\n1712\t\t\t\t\t\t'path segments avoid the characters Windows refuses',\n1713\t\t\t\t\t),\n1714\t\t\t\t)\n1715\t\t\t}\n1716\t\t\tif (segment.endsWith('.') || segment.endsWith(' ')) {\n1717\t\t\t\tviolations.push(\n1718\t\t\t\t\tcreatePolicyViolation(\n1719\t\t\t\t\t\t'portability',\n1720\t\t\t\t\t\tpath,\n1721\t\t\t\t\t\t'path segments end with neither a dot nor a space',\n1722\t\t\t\t\t),\n1723\t\t\t\t)\n1724\t\t\t}\n1725\t\t\tconst key = path.toLowerCase()\n1726\t\t\tconst previous = folded.get(key)\n1727\t\t\tif (previous === undefined) {\n1728\t\t\t\tfolded.set(key, path)\n1729\t\t\t} else if (previous !== path) {\n1730\t\t\t\tviolations.push(\n1731\t\t\t\t\tcreatePolicyViolation('portability', path, `path differs from ${previous} by case alone`),\n1732\t\t\t\t)\n1733\t\t\t}\n1734\t\t}\n1735\t\treturn violations\n1736\t}\n1737\t\n1738\t/**\n1739\t * Read the workspace-authored path population, directories included.\n1740\t *\n1741\t * @param root - The workspace root to read.\n1742\t * @returns Every authored path, sorted by path.\n1743\t */\n1744\texport function readPolicyPaths(root: string): readonly string[] {"}]},"uuid":"1fc6d58e-7f41-47f2-9ff0-32ce5274c14e","timestamp":"2026-08-27T01:39:55.600Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"733e14bb-628f-4792-ac20-7a676fbe705d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"df6780f2-899a-446b-9107-2dffa463f40e","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a85cb90a15fbe52ec","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CeSQnU955B7MMGVnK4txf","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"## Lane held\n\n**Objective lane** — correctness, constraints, and what the code and contracts actually permit. Dispatched as `planner` on Opus 5 because the Codex bench is dark. Every mechanism below was read in this checkout before it was proposed; the file and line references are what I verified, not what `PROPOSAL.md` asserted.\n\n---\n\n# Design\n\n## The finding that shapes everything else\n\n`PROPOSAL.md:8-21` measures 92 KiB per repository across four files. Reading the loading mechanism for each, the cost decomposes as:\n\n| File | Bytes | Why it loads |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `CLAUDE.md` | 2,844 | Auto-loaded memory file |\n| `AGENTS.md` | 17,950 | Auto-loaded memory file, and `@`-imported by `CLAUDE.md:1` |\n| `.agents/orchestration.md` | 63,388 | Loaded **only** through `@.agents/orchestration.md` at `CLAUDE.md:2` |\n| `.claude/rules/writing.md` | 8,220 | The only rule file with no `paths:` frontmatter |\n\nI verified the last row directly: every file in `.claude/rules/` carries `paths:` at line 2 except `writing.md`, which has no frontmatter at all.\n\nSo the injected set is decided by what the target's `CLAUDE.md` imports and by frontmatter, not by what sits in the target's tree. Replacing `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md` with pointers that import nothing, and removing `.claude/rules`, removes 100% of the measured cost. Nothing else in `HOST_PATHS` contributes a byte to it.\n\nThat does not make the wider cut wrong — the contract graph forces most of it — but it does mean each further removal must be justified by drift or by reference integrity, never by tokens. Two removals fail that test and I refuse them (questions 4 and 5).\n\n## 1. The membership split\n\n`HOST_PATHS` (`src/core/constants.ts:124-159`) drives three things at once: what `stageHost` copies into `dist/host` (`src/server/helpers.ts:1401`), what `stageInventory` records in `host.json`, and what `nameToHostArtifacts` plans into a target (`src/core/compilers.ts:1512-1518`). The migration needs staging to stay wide while planning narrows.\n\n**Split by adding a second constant for the staged-only set, and leave `HOST_PATHS` meaning exactly what its name and every consumer already say it means.**\n\n```ts\n/** The instruction paths the vendored data root carries for reading and never writes into a target, frozen. */\nexport const CANON_PATHS: readonly string[] = Object.freeze([\n\t'AGENTS.md',\n\t'CLAUDE.md',\n\t'.agents/orchestration.md',\n\t'.agents/skills',\n\t'.agents/templates',\n\t'.agents/transports',\n\t'.claude/rules',\n\t'.claude/skills',\n])\n```\n\n`HOST_PATHS` loses exactly those members and keeps everything else. The direction matters: `EXECUTABLE_PATHS`' TSDoc (`:186`) says \"Every entry is also a `HOST_PATHS` member\", `selectHostPaths` filters `HOST_PATHS`, the CLI setup advisory (`src/bin/CLI.ts:1370`) skips vendored `tests/` members of it, and `tests/setupServer.ts` builds fixtures from it. Widening `HOST_PATHS` and narrowing a new plan constant would move every one of those; narrowing `HOST_PATHS` and widening only the producer moves one function.\n\nChanges, exactly:\n\n- **`src/core/constants.ts`** — add `CANON_PATHS`; remove the eight members from `HOST_PATHS`; add `.claude/agents/orkestrel.md` is **not** needed as a separate constant because `.claude/agents` stays a directory member (question 5). Revise `HOST_PATHS`' TSDoc: it is the set written into a target. Revise `CANON_PATHS`' TSDoc: it is staged and never planned, and the pointer names it.\n- **`src/server/helpers.ts` `stageHost`** — iterate `[...HOST_PATHS, ...CANON_PATHS]` at line 1401. Everything downstream (missing-path refusal, storage collision guard, digests, `roots`, `manifest.json`) is unchanged and covers the wider set for free.\n- **`src/core/helpers.ts`** — add `isCanonPath(path: string): boolean`, returning true for a `CANON_PATHS` member or anything beneath one. It sits beside `isDeferredPath` (`:188`) for the reason `architecture.md` § Kind purity states: a bare predicate over a string is a helper, not a `Guard<T>`.\n- **`src/bin/CLI.ts` `#host` (`:632-634`)** — add `&& !isCanonPath(entry.destination)` to the fetch filter.\n- **`src/server/helpers.ts` `filesToHost` (`:1209-1239`)** — treat a canon destination the way a deferred one is treated: retain the installed floor's bytes rather than requiring a live row. Without this pair of edits, every `audit` and `repair` in every target fetches the whole canon set from upstream on every run, because `filesToHost` returns `undefined` unless it holds live bytes for every non-deferred floor entry (`:1222`). It would still be *correct*; it would cost ~92 KiB of network per invocation for bytes no verb writes.\n\n**No type changes.** `ManifestEntry` (`src/server/types.ts:65-70`) carries `storage`, `destination`, `executable`, `digest` and no group; adding a membership field there would store a fact `CANON_PATHS` already decides, which `AGENTS.md` § Design laws forbids (\"Derive state\"). `HostArtifact.source` (`src/core/types.ts:375`) already exists and needs no widening.\n\n## 2. Pointer mechanism and content\n\n**The pointer is a template artifact, not a vendored file.**\n\nThe vendored route cannot express it without new data. Two artifacts want the destination `AGENTS.md`: the canonical file staged for the `node_modules` fallback, and the pointer written into the target. `stageHost:1454-1460` refuses two vendored paths claiming one storage name, and `pathToStorage` is injective, so the two cannot coexist as vendored members at that destination. Making the pointer a vendored member at some other path and remapping it with `HostArtifact.source` works mechanically (`Materializer.#remap`, `:766-777`), but it forces `nameToHostArtifacts` to carry a source-to-path map — new data whose only member is the pointer pair.\n\nA pointer is generated prose whose bytes scaffold owns and fills identically for every target. That is precisely what `ContentArtifact` with `origin: 'template'` is (`src/core/types.ts:398-404`), and `templates.ts` is the file the kind table names for shipped template definitions.\n\n- **`src/core/templates.ts`** — add `ARTIFACT_TEMPLATES.docs.agents` and `ARTIFACT_TEMPLATES.docs.claude` beside the existing `docs.readme` (`:2010-2011`). Neither carries a `{{token}}`; the pointer says nothing target-specific.\n- **`src/core/compilers.ts` `blueprintToDocsArtifacts`** — emit both beside `README.md`, each `{ group: 'docs', ownership: 'content', origin: 'template' }`. `inferGroup` returns `docs` for both paths (`src/core/helpers.ts:225`), so the compiler's group and the audit's group agree.\n\nContent ownership is what makes `PROPOSAL.md:90-92` work: the target's existing full `AGENTS.md` is reported `stale` and `repair` replaces it. That is the same treatment those two paths get today after hydration, so no target's expectations change.\n\n**The pointer prose.** `PROPOSAL.md:58` names `node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/` as the fallback root and implies the paths continue unchanged beneath it. That is false, and it is load-bearing: `pathToStorage` strips the dot that opens each segment (`src/server/helpers.ts:215-221`, and its own example `pathToStorage('.claude/rules/names.md') // 'claude/rules/names.md'`). The staged copies sit at `dist/host/AGENTS.md`, `dist/host/agents/orchestration.md`, and `dist/host/claude/rules/`. The pointer must name those.\n\n`AGENTS.md` in a target:\n\n```markdown\n# AGENTS.md\n\nThe `@orkestrel/scaffold` package is this repository's coding and orchestration authority. This\nfile points at it and states no law of its own.\n\nRead these before working, in order: the `AGENTS.md` coding contract, the\n`.agents/orchestration.md` agent-operation contract, every applicable file in the\n`.claude/rules/` directory named by the rule map in `AGENTS.md`, and the dispatch-named skill\nunder `.agents/skills/` with the references it requires.\n\nResolve every one of those paths against a scaffold checkout, never against this repository:\n\n- When a scaffold checkout sits beside this repository, read `../scaffold/AGENTS.md`,\n  `../scaffold/.agents/orchestration.md`, and the `../scaffold/.claude/rules/` directory.\n- Otherwise read the copy this repository installs: the\n  `node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/AGENTS.md` file, the\n  `node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/agents/orchestration.md` file, and the\n  `node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/claude/rules/` directory. That copy strips the dot\n  that opens each path segment, which is why the names differ.\n\nEvery path a scaffold-supplied file names resolves the same way. The files this repository still\ncarries — the `.claude/agents/` directory, the `.codex/` directory, the `.cursor/` directory, the\n`.mcp.json` file, and the `.claude/settings.json` file — are this repository's own copies and\nresolve here.\n\nEdit none of the scaffold-owned files here. The `scaffold repair` command restores them, so the\nchange process is a commit in the scaffold repository followed by a release.\n```\n\n`CLAUDE.md` in a target:\n\n```markdown\n# CLAUDE.md\n\nRead the `AGENTS.md` file in this repository first. It names the coding and orchestration\nauthority and where to read it.\n\nThis file imports nothing. An `@path` import inlines the imported file into each importing file's\ncontext, which is the cost this pointer removes.\n```\n\nBoth are plain prose with no `@path` import, as `PROPOSAL.md:36-40` requires. The second file states the ban on its own face so a later edit that reintroduces an import meets the reason at the point of the edit.\n\n## 3. `.claude/settings.json`\n\n**Stays vendored.** Three reasons, each checkable:\n\n- It is read by the Claude Code CLI as configuration and is never injected as memory, so it contributes nothing to the measured 335.2k.\n- It is the permissions floor. `CLAUDE.md` § Publishing the fleet already fixes the division: a target's own grants go in `.claude/settings.local.json`, and `repair` restores the vendored `settings.json`. Removing it strands every target's floor with no replacement.\n- It is the only surviving `HOST_PATHS` member under a `.claude`/`.agents`/`.codex`/`.cursor` prefix that is guaranteed in **both** scaffold and every target, which is what the policy re-scope in question 6 needs as its glob-branch anchor.\n\n`matchesSensitivePath('.claude/settings.json')` is `false` (`src/server/helpers.ts:154`), so staging already admits it while refusing `settings.local.json`.\n\n## 4. Bridge files in targets\n\n**A target keeps `.codex/agents`, `.codex/config.toml`, `.cursor/mcp.json`, `.cursor/rules`, and `.mcp.json`, vendored byte-identical, with no content change.**\n\n`PROPOSAL.md:61-64` observes that these already instruct by reference. That observation is the answer: `.codex/config.toml:14` says \"Read AGENTS.md, .agents/orchestration.md, …\". In a migrated target, `AGENTS.md` is the pointer, so the existing byte-identical file already lands a Codex session on the pointer with no edit. The dangling `.agents/orchestration.md` reference is closed by the pointer's path-resolution paragraph.\n\nTwo mechanical facts decide `.mcp.json` against removal. It registers the `probe` server (`.mcp.json:7-10`), and `AGENTS.md` § TTTDD makes calling that server's `prove` tool binding inside every target. It is not a memory file, so it costs nothing. Removing it disables a mandated instrument in every repository to save zero bytes.\n\nWhat a session on a bare target reads:\n\n- **Claude Code** reads `CLAUDE.md`, which sends it to `AGENTS.md`, which sends it to the scaffold checkout. Roles are present because `.claude/agents` stays (question 5). Skills are not; an orchestrating session attaches scaffold, where they are.\n- **Codex** reads `.codex/config.toml` `developer_instructions` and the root `AGENTS.md` pointer. Roles are present because `.codex/agents` stays.\n- **Cursor** reads the `alwaysApply` rule in `.cursor/rules/orchestration.mdc`, which names `AGENTS.md`. Unchanged.\n\n## 5. The catalog agent\n\n**`.claude/agents` stays a planned directory member.** This is forced, not chosen.\n\n`Materializer.catalog` (`:380-390`) delegates to `#rewrite`, which reads the file and throws when it is absent:\n\n```ts\nconst text = readFileText(target, path, limit)\nif (text === undefined) {\n    throw this.#error('TARGET', `The file at ${path} is not readable text.`, { target, path })\n}\n```\n\n`Materializer.ts:951-954`. The catalog verb never creates `.claude/agents/orkestrel.md`; it rewrites a marked region in a file `repair` put there. Removing `.claude/agents` from the planned set therefore makes `scaffold catalog` throw in every target, permanently.\n\nThe minimum fix is to plan `.claude/agents/orkestrel.md` as a file member. I keep the whole directory instead, for three further reasons that the minimum fix loses:\n\n- **Foreign detection and deletion die otherwise.** `Materializer.#roots` (`:639-654`) builds the owned-root set only from host artifacts the plan names, and `#derive` (`:618-629`) extends the snapshot only over those roots. With every directory member gone, `audit` can never report a `foreign` finding and `remove` (`:449-476`) can never delete anything, in any generated workspace. A public verb that can never act is drift the guide would have to document as a limit.\n- `.claude/agents/*.md` files are subagent definitions loaded on dispatch, never injected as memory. They cost nothing.\n- Keeping them removes the largest unmeasured capability risk in the change: whether `--add-dir ../scaffold` registers roles in a target session is undocumented, and this makes the answer not matter for roles.\n\n`isDeferredPath` already keeps `orkestrel.md` presence-owned through hydration (`Materializer.ts:680-682`), so a consumer's own edits outside the markers survive `repair`, and `#host` already excludes it from the live fill (`CLI.ts:633`). None of that changes.\n\n`.codex/agents` stays for the symmetric reason on the Codex side.\n\n## 6. Policy-test re-scope\n\nMeasured against the code, exactly two assertions in the vendored `tests/policy.test.ts` fail in a target whose canon trees are absent:\n\n- `:349-351` — `readSkillFamily(process.cwd())` must be non-empty and contain `orkestrel-falsify`.\n- `:496` — `readPolicyPaths(process.cwd())` must contain `.claude/rules/names.md`.\n\nEverything else passes on absence, and I confirmed each: `inspectSkillFamily` and `inspectSkillBridges` return `[]` when both roots are absent (`setupPolicy.ts:1033-1034`, `:1592-1623`); `inspectPolicyRuleMap` returns `[]` when `.claude/rules` does not resolve (`:1654-1655`); `inspectPolicyWorkspace` (`:1943-1951`) composes only inspectors that pass on absence.\n\n**The re-scope, per assertion:**\n\n- **`:349-351`** → assert the relationship instead of the literal: `readSkillFamily` returns a non-empty family exactly when the family root holds at least one subdirectory, read directly with `readdirSync`. That binds in scaffold (the root holds directories, so an empty answer fails) and passes in a target (the root is absent, so a non-empty answer fails). It compares the function's answer against a second mechanism that can disagree, which is what `.claude/rules/tests.md` requires and what a bare `if (family.length === 0) return` would not give.\n- **`:496`** → replace `.claude/rules/names.md` with `.claude/settings.json`. The case is named \"reaches every branch of the workspace-authored path population\" and its four literals exist to cover the four branches of `POLICY_PORTABILITY_GLOB` (`setupPolicy.ts:272-277`). `.claude/settings.json` covers the `{.agents,.claude,.codex,.cursor,.github}/**/*` branch and is guaranteed by `HOST_PATHS` in scaffold and in every target. The other three literals are unaffected.\n\n**One thing is deliberately lost.** The `orkestrel-falsify` membership assertion does not move to another file. It is scaffold canon, not a policy law a generated workspace owes, and re-homing it would require either a new cross-cutting proof (`tests.md` forbids inventing one) or reading the workspace's own package name inside a byte-identical vendored file (which would put scaffold's identity into every target's test). What that assertion actually guarded — a discovery function silently returning empty — is fully carried by the replacement. Record it in the matrix as **intentionally excluded, with reason**.\n\nA scaffold-only registration is the wrong seam here for a general reason worth stating: a vendored file that branches on which repository it is running in has two behaviours and one set of tests, and the branch nobody runs is the one that rots.\n\n**One further mechanical obligation.** Once `.claude/rules` leaves a target while `AGENTS.md` becomes a pointer, the pair must move together. `inspectPolicyRuleMap` reports every rule file that no `## Rule map` row names (`setupPolicy.ts:1666-1669`), and the pointer carries no rule map. Keeping `.claude/rules` vendored while pointer-izing `AGENTS.md` therefore reddens the vendored suite in every target. This is why `.claude/rules` is not optional membership: mechanism forces it out.\n\n## 7. The orphan sweep\n\n**The visit documents a manual `git rm -r`. Scaffold ships no mechanism.**\n\n`repair` writes only `missing` and `stale` (`Materializer.ts:305-313`) and never deletes. The obvious alternative — let `overwrite`/`remove` take them — cannot work: `remove` deletes only tracked `foreign` findings, and a `foreign` finding is produced only for a file beneath a root the plan expands (`#roots`, `:639-654`). The moment `.agents` and `.claude/rules` leave the plan, files there stop being foreign and become invisible. Making them reachable would require a new \"roots scaffold owns but plans nothing in\" concept, which is machinery built for a one-release event.\n\nWidening `repair` to delete is refused outright. Its contract is the narrowest of the writing verbs, and deletion sits behind `remove`'s clean-tree, git-tracked, audit-agreement guards for reasons the type documentation states at `src/core/types.ts:438-462`.\n\n`AGENTS.md` § Design laws settles it: \"Mechanism, not product policy. Framework code supplies reusable mechanisms and stops before application decisions.\" *Delete last release's instruction copies* is this release's policy. `git rm -r` is git-recoverable, runs once per target, and rides in the visit that already re-pins and repairs.\n\n**The sweep is load-bearing, not tidiness.** A target visited without it keeps `.claude/rules/writing.md`, which has no `paths:` frontmatter and therefore keeps auto-loading its 8,220 bytes. The wave step must name the exact paths and prove the removal.\n\nHome for the step: `.agents/skills/orkestrel-publish/references/wave.md`, in the per-repo visit, plus the migration paragraph in `guides/scaffold.md`.\n\n## 8. Membership edges\n\n| Path | Ruling | Reason |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `scripts/*.sh` | Tool surface, stays | Executed by a session, never read into context. They are the whole of `EXECUTABLE_PATHS` (`constants.ts:188-193`), so removing them would leave the executable-bit path with no production subject. |\n| `.agents/templates` | Canon, leaves | `.agents/templates/brief.md` is named by `.agents/orchestration.md` § Check the brief before you send it. It is read only while writing a brief, and a session writing one has scaffold attached. |\n| `.agents/transports` | Canon, leaves | Bound by reference from the role files' charters; it travels with the contract that names it. |\n| `LICENSE` | Tool surface, stays | Zero context cost; every published package needs its own copy. |\n| `guides/guide.md` | Tool surface, stays | The `@orkestrel/guide` contract each package writes its guides against. Not a memory file. |\n| `guides/scaffold.md` | Tool surface, stays | How a target uses the CLI. `selectHostPaths` already drops it for scaffold itself (`core/helpers.ts:384-387`). |\n| `.agents/skills` + `.claude/skills` | Canon, leave **together** | `inspectSkillBridges` fails when one side is present and the other absent (`setupPolicy.ts:1598-1620`). They cannot be split. |\n\n## 9.
